### Part 3: 计算聚合 DAF

对已完成的 tasks，计算聚合 DAF：
- δ = mean(int_DR) - mean(det_DR)
- Δ_nd = 需要从 non-death episodes 计算（如果数据可得的话，否则基于 D/I/N 标注估算）
- DAF = 1 - Δ_nd / (δ × P + Δ_nd)，P=100

````text
Perfect! Now I have all the information. Let me create a comprehensive summary:

## Summary: DAF (Death Avoidance Factor) Definition and Formula

Based on my search through the SimGate project codebase, I've found the complete definition and implementation of DAF. Here's what I discovered:

### 1. **The Exact Formula**

From `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_12/projects/simgate-modular-routing/artifacts/_project/paper/latex/method.tex`:

```
DAF = 1 - Δ_nd / Δ_full
```

### 2. **Term Definitions**

- **Δ_full** (Delta_full): The mean score gap between deterministic and internal strategies **across all episodes**
  - Formula: `Δ_full = mean(s^det) - mean(s^int)`
  - Where `s^det` = final scores from deterministic (environment simulator) strategy
  - Where `s^int` = final scores from pure internal (LLM reasoning) strategy
  - Computed over ALL episodes including those with deaths

- **Δ_nd** (Delta_no_death): The mean score gap between strategies **excluding episodes where either strategy died**
  - Formula: `Δ_nd = mean(s^det | neither dead) - mean(s^int | neither dead)`
  - Only includes episode pairs where BOTH strategies survived (both scores > -100)
  - Episodes where either strategy died are excluded from this calculation

- **Death Threshold**: A score of -100 indicates agent death in both environments (ScienceWorld and ALFWorld)

### 3. **Computation from Episode Data**

From `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_12/projects/simgate-modular-routing/src/death_decomposition.py` and `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_12/projects/simgate-modular-routing/src/routing_pattern_analysis.py`:

**Step 1: Load episode scores**
```python
int_scores = [ep["always_internal"]["final_score"] for ep in episodes]
det_scores = [ep["always_deterministic"]["final_score"] for ep in episodes]
```

**Step 2: Identify no-death subset (non-death episodes)**
```python
nd_int_scores = []
nd_det_scores = []
for i_s, d_s in zip(int_scores, det_scores):
    if not is_dead(i_s) and not is_dead(d_s):  # neither dead (score > -100)
        nd_int_scores.append(i_s)
        nd_det_scores.append(d_s)
```

**Step 3: Compute deltas**
```python
delta_full = mean(det_scores) - mean(int_scores)  # all episodes
delta_nd = mean(nd_det_scores) - mean(nd_int_scores)  # no-death subset only
```

**Step 4: Compute DAF**
```python
if delta_full != 0 and not isnan(delta_nd):
    daf = (delta_full - delta_nd) / delta_full
else:
    daf = NaN
```

### 4. **Interpretation**

The pipeline figure shows: `Δ_full ≈ δP + Δ_nd` where:
- **δP** represents the "death penalty" contribution (the component of the gap attributable to death asymmetry)
- **Δ_nd** represents genuine performance improvement (score gap when both survive)
- **DAF** = fraction of the total performance gap attributable to death avoidance
  - DAF ≈ 1.0 means almost all the performance gap is due to death asymmetry (death-driven)
  - DAF ≈ 0.0 means the gap is genuine performance improvement (non-death-driven)

### 5. **Related Metric: Death Avoidance Fraction**

The code also computes an equivalent metric called "death_avoidance_frac":
```python
death_avoidance_frac = (delta_full - delta_nd) / delta_full
```
This is identical to the DAF formula and represents the same concept.

### 6. **Key Source Files**

- **Definition & Formula**: `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_12/projects/simgate-modular-routing/artifacts/_project/paper/latex/method.tex` (lines 22-29)
- **Implementation (primary)**: `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_12/projects/simgate-modular-routing/src/death_decomposition.py` (lines 140-143)
- **Alternative implementation**: `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_12/projects/simgate-modular-routing/src/routing_pattern_analysis.py` (lines 52-60)
- **Comparison analysis**: `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_12/projects/simgate-modular-routing/scripts/matched_daf_comparison.py` (lines 69-69)
````

Great, now I understand DAF:
- DAF = 1 - Δ_nd / Δ_full
- Δ_full = mean(det_scores) - mean(int_scores) over all episodes
- Δ_nd = mean(det_scores | neither dead) - mean(int_scores | neither dead) over non-death subset
- DAF near 1.0 = gap mainly due to death asymmetry
- DAF near 0.0 = gap is genuine performance difference

And melt is now at 11/20 episodes (growing). 

Now I need to gather the raw episode data for all four completed tasks to compute the DAF values. The user wants the LaTeX table regardless of whether melt finishes, so I should pull the episode summaries for boil and the other three tasks to get the scores I need for the calculation. Looking at just the non-death episodes where neither character died, I'm filtering down to the cases where both stayed alive to see if that subset shows a different pattern. So boil shows a DAF of 1.0, meaning the entire performance gap comes from the death asymmetry between policies—they're equivalent in episodes where both agents survive. Now I need to pull the raw data for the remaining three tasks and check if melt has been updated to 11 episodes.

### find-living-thing (20 episodes)
Let me recount the internal deaths from the episode data. Looking at the scores, I need to identify which episodes have -100 values and verify the death rate calculation against the detective deaths. Generating markdown table... Now I'm looking at the preference data across all twenty cases and calculating the death rates—internal deaths account for half the population while deterministic preferences only cause twenty percent, giving me a thirty-point differential that checks out.

Generating preference table... I've verified all the user data and calculated the death rate difference at 35 percentage points. Now I'm computing the DAF scores for each task by gathering the internal and deterministic score arrays.

I'm working through the Boil task's calculations, summing up the internal scores to get -1094 with a mean of -54.7, then recounting the deterministic scores more carefully to confirm the sum is -424 after accounting for the positive values (76), negative values (-500), and zeros.

Now I'm calculating the mean for deterministic scores as -21.2, which gives me a difference of 33.5 points between the two scoring methods. I'm also filtering out the episodes where neither score hit -100 to look at just the non-death episodes, which gives me 9 episodes to analyze separately.

For those non-death episodes, both scoring methods average to 0.667, so there's no difference there. This means the Divergence Adjustment Factor comes out to 1.0, indicating perfect alignment on the non-death cases.

Now I'm working through the find-living-thing category with a new set of scores, calculating that the mean intensity score is -54.2. Now I'm calculating the mean for the determinant scores and finding the difference for non-death episodes, which comes out to about 1.889. Using that, I can compute the DAF metric as 0.954. Moving on to the use-thermometer data, I'm summing up the integrity and determinant scores across all 20 episodes to get their respective means. Continuing to filter out episodes where either metric shows a death marker, I'm left with nine valid episodes to analyze for the non-death subset. Now I'm calculating the mean scores for the power component across all episodes—the integrity scores average to -50, while the determinism scores come to -11.5, giving a delta of 38.5. Then I'm filtering for non-death episodes where neither score is -100 to compute the differential average. Now I'm aggregating the DAF metric across all four tasks by summing the integrity and detriment scores, then calculating the mean values to get an overall aggregate delta and DAF score.

For the no-preference episodes, I'm computing the mean integrity and detriment separately, then finding the aggregate delta as the difference between those means, which gives me a DAF of 0.987.

Now I'm moving on to analyze the melt dataset with its 11 episodes, looking at the integrity and detriment scores along with the preference labels.

Compiling the melt data... Finishing the DAF calculation: 0/45.73 equals 1.0, so melt also has a DAF of 1.0. Since melt is still running at 11/20 episodes and not yet complete, I should mark it with "—" in the table and use the 4-task aggregate instead of trying to calculate its individual metrics. Let me double-check the user's instructions about how to handle incomplete tasks.

Now I'm compiling the aggregate metrics across the four completed tasks to build the summary table. Now I'm verifying the totals across all four tasks check out to 80 episodes, then calculating the per-task DAF values to include in the output alongside the aggregate results.
